  • With over 60% indigenous content, ICGS Samudra Pratap is India’s 昀椀rst indigenously designed pollution control vessel and the largest ship in the ICG 昀氀eet to date.
  • The report was launched on 17 November 2025 during COP30 in Belém, Brazil.

UNEP states the GMP includes & Related Developments

  • UNEP states the GMP includes 159 countries + the European Commission.
  • The report notes that (as of April 2025) those members covered about 57% of global anthropogenic methane emissions.
  • Sector shares of global anthropogenic methane are about 42% agriculture, 38% energy, 20% waste.
  • G20+ countries are responsible for about 65% of total anthropogenic methane emissions and hold 72% of global technicale methane mitigation potential by 2030. 30th Conference of the Parties to the UNFCCC: COP30 COP30, held in Belém in the Amazon region, became strongly associated with an “implementation-昀椀rst” framing. Alongside the political track, COP30 also showcased a large- scale implementation architecture through the COP30 Global Climate Action Agenda (GCAA)—a renewed framework built on the Marrakech Partnership and the Global Stocktake response, organized into six thematic axes and driven by 30 activation groups (482 initiatives).  Key Highlights
  • COP30 took place in Belem from November 10-22, 2025.
  • In parallel, the COP30 Global Climate Action Agenda (GCAA) operationalized this “implem- entation turn” through a four-step activation cycle–Coordinate, Measure, Showcase, Scale.
  • Coordinate: 30 activation groups with 482 initiatives aligned toward 30 key objectives.  COP30’s “Implementation Shift” and the Belém Package
  • Unlike earlier COPs that leaned heavily toward announcing new pledges, COP30 marked a clear shift toward implementation.
  • A two: year “Climate Finance Work Programme” was launched.
  • To curb emissions and accelerate system transitions, COP30 advanced the implementation of the Belém 4x Pledge. The pledge was launched at the Pre- COP .  Target: quadruple the use of sustainable fuels by 2035 relative to 2024 (including green hydrogen, advanced biofuels, and e-fuels).  Tracking: IEA to track progress.
  • The Fossil Fuel Treaty Initiative announced its 18th nation-state supporter (Cambodia) and presented a PAS with next steps through the Climate Action Agenda.  Health
  • Belém Health Action Plan: launched to build climate-resilient health systems to tackle heatwaves and vector-borne diseases.
  • The Belém Health Action Plan is described as the world’s 昀椀rst international climate adaptation plan dedicated entirely to health , launched with an initial USD 300 million investment from a coalition of 35+ philanthropies, funding research, policy, solutions for extreme heat/air pollution/ infectious diseases, and strengthening health systems via climate-data integration.

  • The Uttar Pradesh Budget for 2026: 27 was presented by Finance Minister Suresh Kumar Khanna on February 11, 2026 . This is a large, expansionary budget with a strong focus on growth, infrastructure, welfare, and technology-led development.
  • The budget combines higher expenditure , continued revenue surplus , and a fiscal deficit kept at 3% of GSDP, which indicates that the state is trying to balance development spending with fiscal discipline.  Economic Background of Uttar Pradesh
  • The Gross State Domestic Product (GSDP) of Uttar Pradesh for 2026–27, at current prices, is projected at ₹39.8 lakh crore . This implies a 28% increase over the revised estimate for 2025–26.
  • The structure of the state’s economy in 2024–25 shows that agriculture contributes 27% , manufacturing contributes 26% , and services contribute 47% of the economy. This means services remain the largest sector, but agriculture continues to hold a very significant share in Uttar Pradesh’s economy.
  • The total expenditure proposed in the budget for 2026–27 is ₹9,12,696 crore . This is 27% higher than the revised estimate for 2025–26.
  • Out of this, debt repayment is estimated at ₹61,795 crore. Therefore, net expenditure , which is the more meaningful measure for policy analysis, stands at ₹8,50,901 crore.
  • Total receipts are budgeted at ₹8,48,233 crore , while net receipts excluding borrowings are estimated at ₹7,32,420 crore. The difference is to be partly met through borrowings.   • The budget estimates a revenue surplus of ₹64,458 crore, which is 1.6% of GSDP , in 2026–27. This means the state expects its revenue receipts to exceed its revenue expenditure.
  • However, this revenue surplus is lower than the revised estimate for 2025–26, when revenue surplus was estimated at ₹74,348 crore, or 2.4% of GSDP. This suggests that while the state remains in surplus on the revenue account, the cushion is becoming smaller.
  • The fiscal deficit for 2026: 27 is budgeted at ₹1,18,481 crore, which is 3% of GSDP. This is the same ratio as the revised estimate for 2025–26 and aligns with the 16th Finance Commission’s recommended annual limit of 3% of GSDP for states for 2026–31.
  • The primary deficit is estimated at ₹49,560 crore, or 1.2% of GSDP, in 2026–27. This is significantly higher than the revised estimate for 2025–26, indicating a larger gap even before interest payments are considered.  Expenditure Pattern
  • Revenue expenditure in 2026: 27 is proposed at ₹6,64,471 crore , which is 31% higher than the revised estimate for 2025–26. Revenue expenditure mainly includes salaries, pensions, interest payments, grants, and subsidies.
  • Capital outlay is budgeted at ₹1,77,744 crore , which is an increase of 11% over the revised estimate of 2025–26. Capital outlay is important because it creates assets such as roads, buildings, and infrastructure.
  • Revenue receipts for 2026: 27 are estimated at ₹7,28,928 crore , which is 26% higher than the revised estimate for 2025–26.
  • State’s own tax revenue is estimated at ₹3,34,491 crore in 2026–27, which is 48% higher than the revised estimate for 2025–26.
  • State’s own non: tax revenue is estimated at ₹26,754 crore , which is almost double the revised estimate for 2025–26. The budget attributes this mainly to increases in concession fees, rent, royalty, share capital receipts from Uttar Pradesh Power Corporation for rural electrification, and interest income.
  • The state’s share in central taxes is estimated at ₹2,68,911 crore, while grants from the Centre are estimated at ₹98,772 crore.  Tax Revenue and Tax Trends
  • Among own tax sources, State GST is projected to be the largest source , contributing 45% of the state’s own tax revenue in 2026–27. State GST is budgeted at ₹1,49,956 crore, which is 79% higher than the revised estimate for 2025–26.
  • State excise is estimated at ₹71,278 crore, or 17% higher than the revised estimate for 2025–26.

Sales tax/VAT is projected at & Related Developments

  • Sales tax/VAT is projected at ₹48,115 crore , showing a 43% increase over the revised estimate of 2025–26.
  • Stamp duty and registration fees are estimated at ₹43,802 crore, while taxes on vehicles are budgeted at ₹15,808 crore.
  • The revised estimates for most own: tax revenue heads in 2025–26 were lower than the original budget estimates, which suggests that revenue projections have recently been difficult to realise fully.  Debt, Liabilities, and Guarantees
  • At the end of 2026: 27, the state’s outstanding liabilities are estimated at 23.1% of GSDP, which is lower than the revised estimate of 27.6% of GSDP for 2025–26. This indicates an expected improvement in the debt burden relative to the size of the economy. Rupee Comes In, 2026-27 (B.E.) Rupee Goes Out, 2026-27 (B.E.)   • According to quick estimates at current prices, the Gross State Domestic Product (GSDP) of Uttar Pradesh increased from `13.30 lakh crore in 2016– 17 to `26.67 lakh crore in 2023–24.
  • In 2024: 25, advance estimates placed it at `29.62 lakh crore , while revised estimates placed it at `29.78 lakh crore.
  • With a 13.4% growth over the previous year , the GSDP is estimated at `30.25 lakh crore , higher than both earlier estimates.
  • In 2025: 26, the state economy is estimated to reach approximately `36 lakh crore.
  • According to the research report ‘Ecowrap’ by State Bank of India , India is moving towards becoming the third-largest economy in the world by 2027, surpassing Japan and Germany, and Uttar Pradesh is expected to play a decisive role in this achievement.
  • Uttar Pradesh’s GSDP at current prices increased from `13.30 lakh crore in 2016–17 to `30.25 lakh crore in 2024–25 (quick estimates) , registering a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 10.8% over 8 years.

From 2017 & Related Developments

  • From 2017: 18 to 2024–25 , structural changes are visible in the state economy:  The primary sector remained almost stable (from 25.9% to 25.8%).  The secondary sector increased significantly (from 25.4% to 27.2% ), indicating growth in industrial production, manufacturing, and infrastructure.  The tertiary sector declined slightly (from 48.8% to 47.0%), indicating a balanced restructuring of services.  Comparison with National Economy
  • At the national level:  The Primary sector declined from 20.4% to 19.7%  The Secondary sector declined from 27.0% to 25.3%  The Tertiary sector increased from 52.5% to 54.9% Sector-wise Contribution in 2024–25 Sub-sector Share Agriculture, forestry and 昀椀sheries 24.9% Construction 12.9% Real estate and professional services 13.9% Manufacturing 11.7% Trade and hotels 8.3% Transport and communication 7.4%  Agriculture and Allied Sector
  • The agriculture and allied sector has shown continuous progress.
  • Its Gross Value Added increased from `2.96 lakh crore in 2016–17 to `6.95 lakh crore in 2024–25 , growing at a CAGR of 11.2% , which is nearly a doubling of value.
  • Advance estimates placed it at `6.86 lakh crore , while revised estimates placed it at `7.12 lakh crore.
  • Crops remain the main component , contributing the highest share of 67.4% in the Gross Value Added (GVA) of the agriculture and allied sector.
  • Livestock contributes 24.7%, forestry contributes 4.5%, and fisheries and aquaculture contribute 3.5%.
  • In 2024: 25, the total foodgrain production in the state increased by 9.9% over the previous year , reaching 737.4 lakh metric tonnes , and the state continues to hold a leading position in the country.
  • Milk production increased by 33.6%, placing the state at the first position in the country.
  • The Gross Value Added (GVA) of the secondary sector increased from `3.34 lakh crore in 2016–17 to `7.60 lakh crore in 2024–25, growing at a CAGR of 10.8%.
  • According to the Annual Survey of Industries 2023–24, the Gross Value Added of industries in the state increased by 25% over the previous year, which is the highest among major states and more than double the national growth rate of 11.9%.  Infrastructure Development
  • In Varanasi, the country’s first urban ropeway project is under construction, which is considered a major step in urban transport.
  • With the start of operation at Noida International Airport at Jewar (Gautam Buddha Nagar), the state will become the first in India with 5 international airports.
  • The state also ranked first in the country in terms of the number of internet subscribers, with 13.82 crore subscribers as of September 2025–26.  Service Sector
  • The Gross Value Added (GVA) of the service sector increased from `5.95 lakh crore in 2016–17 to `13.10 lakh crore in 2024–25 (quick estimates) , growing at a CAGR of 10.4%.
  • The total state budget increased from `3.47 lakh crore in 2016–17 to `8.33 lakh crore in 2025–26 , recording an increase of `4.86 lakh crore over nine years.

The per capita income (net) & Related Developments

  • The per capita income (net) increased from `54,564 in 2016–17 to `1,09,844 in 2024–25 , growing at a CAGR of about 9.1%.
  • During the period 2020: 21 to 2024–25, the growth rate was higher at 14.7%.
  • The per capita income (gross) was estimated at `1,26,304 in 2024–25.  Population and Demographic Trends
  • As per March 2026 estimates, Uttar Pradesh has a population of 25.19 crore, which is:  17.8% of India’s population (141.51 crore)  3.0% of the world’s population (830 crore)
  • India contributes about 17% of the global population.
  • If Uttar Pradesh were a country, it would rank 5th in the world in population , after India, China, the United States (33.5 crore), and Indonesia (27.7 crore).  Population Growth and Fertility
  • The population growth rate declined from 1.85% per year in 2011 to 1.70% in 2021, and is expected to decline further to 0.58% during 2026–2051.
  • The Total Fertility Rate (TFR) declined from 2.7 (NFHS-4, 2015–16) to 2.4 (NFHS-5, 2019–21) and is expected to reach the replacement level of 2.1 soon.  Life Expectancy and Social Indicators
  • The State’s Own Tax Revenue increased from `0.86 lakh crore in 2016–17 to `2.09 lakh crore in 2024–25 (advance estimates), which is about 2.5 times growth.
  • The capital expenditure increased from `69.79 thousand crore in 2016–17 to `147.72 thousand crore in 2024–25 (advance estimates) , reflecting development-oriented priorities.
  • Over the last nine years, the total budget increased at a CAGR of about 10.3%, reaching approximately `8.09 lakh crore in 2025–26.
  • During the same period, total receipts increased at 10.2% CAGR, reaching about `7.79 lakh crore.
  • As per the 2025: 26 budget:  Revenue expenditure: `5.83 lakh crore
  • Revenue receipts increased from `2.57 lakh crore (2016–17) to `6.63 lakh crore (2025–26 BE).
  • State Tax Revenue  2016: 17: `0.86 lakh crore  2025–26: `2.95 lakh crore  Growth rate: CAGR of 14.7%
  • Share in Central Taxes  Estimated at `2.55 lakh crore in 2025–26  Growth rate: 9.9%
  • Non: Tax Revenue  State non-tax revenue: `0.25 lakh crore (2025–26)  Grants from the Centre: increased at a CAGR of 11.7% to about `0.88 lakh crore (2025–26).

Chapter 8

Bihar Current Affairs

Bihar state current developments, data centres, GSVA growth rates, agriculture milestones, and governance portals.

 Bihar’s First AI & Related Developments

  •  Bihar’s First AI: Ready Data Center
  • Chief minister Samrat Choudhary on October 13,2026 inaugurated a Rs 400-crore data centre of CtrlS Data centers Ltd in Patliputra industrial area in Patna with an initial IT load capacity of 12 MW .
  • Along with it CtrlS Data Centers has also signed a letter of intent (LoI) with the Bihar government to invest ₹10,000 crore in a proposed 150 MW hyper- scale AI factory in Bodh Gaya.  Sankalp Dashboard and Samanvay Portal
  • Recently on October 13,2026, Bihar chief minister launched the Sankalp Dashboard and Samanvay Portal.
  • The Sankalp dashboard brings the work of all 48 state departments onto one digital platform to improve transparency and accountability.  Cabinet clears Bihar’s 昀椀rst tech park at Gardanibagh, Patna
  • The state cabinet on 12 October ,2026 approved Bihar’s first tech park at Gardanibagh in Patna, to anchor an IT and IT-enabled services (ITES) ecosystem in the capital.
  • NBCC (India) Ltd, a central public sector enterprise, will build it, and the state is authorised to sign an MoU with it.  Bihar MSME Policy, 2026
  • The policy was unveiled on 8 October 2026 , alongside the inauguration of MSME centres in all districts to support entrepreneurs.
  • Major Targets in the Draft:  Formalise 1 crore MSMEs across manufacturing and services.  Generate over 1 crore direct and indirect jobs.  Integrate 500-plus MSMEs into Global Value Chains (GVCs).  Train 1 lakh MSMEs annually in Industry 4.0 and sector-specific skills.  Establish four Technology/Extension Centres in Kosi, Bhagalpur, Munger and Purnea.
  • Incentives Proposed in the Draft:  Capital subsidy: Provide 25% in Category A districts and 30% in Category B districts, capped at ₹25 lakh, ₹1.5 crore and ₹5 crore for micro, small and medium enterprises, respectively.  Additional support: Provide 10% additional capital subsidy to women, SC, ST, BC, Divyang and transgender entrepreneurs.  Employment support: Reimburse employers’ EPF contributions for three years, capped at ₹24,000 per employee annually, subject to employment thresholds.  Electricity subsidy: Reimburse 20% of eligible low- tension power charges for three years.  Rooftop solar subsidy: Provide 25% of installation cost, capped at ₹5 lakh, for systems up to 300 kW .  Stamp duty: Provide 100% reimbursement on land purchases by new manufacturing enterprises. Note: These detailed targets and incentives are from the July 2026 draft.  Rising Bihar - Investor Summit 2026

Bihar’s Budget for 2026–27 was presented by Finance Minister Bijendra Prasad Yadav on February 3, 2026.  Key Highlights
  • The size of Bihar’s budget has increased from `23,885 crore in 2004–05 to `3,47,589.76 crore (including debt repayment) in 2026–27.
  • For 2026: 27, Bihar’s net budget expenditure is estimated at `3,47,589.76 crore, which is `30,694.74 crore higher than the Budget Estimate of `3,16,895.02 crore for 2025–26.
  • The annual scheme outlay is `1,22,155.42 crore , while establishment and committed expenditure is `2,25,434.34 crore.
  • The Gross State Domestic Product (GSDP) for 2026–27 is projected at about `13.09 lakh crore, showing an estimated growth of about 15% over 2025–26.
  • The Budget targets a revenue surplus of `1,143.19 crore and a fiscal deficit of `39,111.80 crore, equal to 2.99% of GSDP, which is within the 3% limit set under the Bihar Fiscal Responsibility and Budget Management framework.
  • The expenditure excluding debt repayment is estimated at `3,24,925 crore, while debt repayment is about `22,665 crore.
  • In the Appropriation Bill, the state proposes gross expenditure of `3,53,045.15 crore, out of which `3,02,242.13 crore is voted expenditure and `50,803.02 crore is charged expenditure.
  • The net expenditure figure used in the budget summary is `3,47,589.76 crore.  Revenue Receipts and Borrowings
  • Total revenue receipts for 2026: 27 are estimated at `2,85,277.12 crore. Of this, the state is expected to raise `75,202.99 crore from its own tax and non-tax sources, while about `2,10,074 crore will come from the Centre through tax devolution and grants.
  • The state’s own tax revenue is estimated at `65,800 crore.
  • Important components include commercial taxes of `50,000 crore, stamp and registration fees of `10,000 crore, transport tax of `5,000 crore, and land revenue of `800 crore.
  • State GST as the largest source of Bihar’s own tax revenue, with an estimated `38,000 crore in 2026–27.
  • The state’s own non: tax revenue is estimated at `9,402.99 crore , including `4,200 crore from mining and `2,570.08 crore from interest receipts.
  • Bihar’s share in central taxes is estimated at `1,58,178.32 crore , while grants-in-aid from the Centre are estimated at `51,895.81 crore.
  • These grants include `44,002.81 crore for Centrally Sponsored Schemes and `7,893 crore linked to Finance Commission-related grants , including disaster response and local body grants.
  • Borrowing of `61,939.48 crore is proposed for 2026–27.
  • The state also proposes to provide loans and advances of `1,413.94 crore.
  • Debt repayment is estimated at `22,664.83 crore , including repayment of loans taken earlier from the Centre and internal loans.  Capital and Revenue Expenditure
  • Total capital expenditure is estimated at `63,455.84 crore, which is 18.26% of total expenditure.
  • This includes capital outlay of `39,377.06 crore , with `6,117.30 crore for general services, `7,434.80 crore for social services and `25,824.96 crore for economic services.

Total revenue expenditure is estimated & Related Developments

  • Total revenue expenditure is estimated at `2,84,133.92 crore, or 81.74% of total expenditure.
  • This includes expenditure on salaries, pensions, interest payments, subsidies, grants and welfare schemes.
  • Under the state’s broader budget classification, spending on salaries, pensions, interest payments and debt repayment together is estimated at `1,79,327.48 crore.
  • Using the comparable committed: expenditure classification of salaries, pensions and interest payments, estimates committed expenditure at `1,33,494 crore, or 47% of revenue receipts.  Bihar’s Economy: Key Indicators
  • Bihar’s economy is projected to grow strongly in nominal terms. For 2026–27, GSDP at current prices is projected at `13.09 lakh crore.
  • In 2024: 25, Bihar’s GSDP at constant prices is estimated to have grown by 8.6%, compared to India’s GDP growth of 6.5%.
  • At current prices, agriculture, manufacturing and services are estimated to contribute 22.37%, 23.57% and 54.07% respectively to Bihar’s economy in 2024–25.
  • Bihar’s per capita GSDP at current prices for 2024– 25 is estimated at `76,490, while India’s per capita GDP is estimated at `2,34,859. Bihar Budget 2026–27
  • Foodgrain production has increased more than three times since 2005. According to the final estimates for 2024–25, total foodgrain production reached 326.62 lakh metric tonnes, the highest ever for the state.
  • Bihar holds an important position in several agricultural products. In 2024–25, it ranked first in makhana and litchi production , second in maize, fourth in honey, fifth in rice and sixth in wheat.
  • The government is working on an AI: enabled Mechanised Agriculture Mission , a Bihar Agri Infrastructure Mission with an initial investment vision of `1 lakh crore , and a Bihar Agriculture Acceleration Mission to promote agri-startups, GI-tagged products and rural agricultural markets.
  • Women’s empowerment is one of the central themes of the Budget. Under Jeevika, about 1.40 crore families have been connected through 11.45 lakh self-help groups , and 91 lakh members have received insurance coverage. At present, 31.71 lakh Jeevika Didis have been declared Lakhpati Didis.
  • For social justice, `19,603.02 crore has been allocated for Scheduled Castes and `1,648.42 crore for Scheduled Tribes in 2026–27.
  • Bihar has maintained the first position on the Government of India’s DVDMS Central Dashboard for supply and distribution of medicines since September 2024.
  • According to the Bihar Economic Survey, 2025–26, Bihar’s Gross State Domestic Product (GSDP) at constant (2011–12) prices grew by 8.6 percent in 2024–25 (Q.E.), and at current prices by 13.1 percent, reaching `5,31,372 crore and `9,91,997 crore respectively.

Per capita GSDP at current & Related Developments

  • Per capita GSDP at current prices increased from `46,412 in 2020–21 to `76,490 in 2024–25, indicating a consistent upward trend.
  • At constant (2011: 12) prices, per capita GSDP increased from `30,159 in 2020–21 to `40,973 in 2024–25.
  • Within Bihar, Patna district recorded the highest Gross District Domestic Product (GDDP), followed by Begusarai and Munger districts.
  • At current prices, per capita GDDP was `2,41,220 for Patna, `1,05,600 for Begusarai, and `93,921 for Munger.  Public Financial Management
  • Bihar’s fiscal development path reflects disciplined financial management, as the state government’s total expenditure increased from `1.66 lakh crore in 2020–21 to `2.82 lakh crore in 2024–25. The state government maintained fiscal prudence through moderate borrowing and effective debt servicing while consistently prioritising developmental expenditure in sectors such as education, health, energy, transport, irrigation, and agriculture.
  • This structural change reflects the government’s focus on long-term investment, infrastructure development, and asset creation.
  • Revenue receipts of the state government increased from `1.28 lakh crore in 2020–21 to `2.18 lakh crore in 2024–25, with tax revenue as the main source. The share of tax revenue increased from 70 percent to 84 percent, while the share of non-tax revenue declined from 5 percent to 2 percent and the share of grants-in-aid declined from 25 percent to 14 percent.
  • The structure of revenue expenditure remained largely stable over the last five years, with social services having the highest share at 46 percent. The share of economic services increased slightly from 21 percent in 2020-21 to 22 percent in 2024-25, while the share of general services declined slightly from 33 percent in 2020-21 to 32 percent in 2024- 25.
  • Developmental expenditure remained around two- thirds of the total budget, reaching 67.4 percent in 2024–25. It increased from `1,12,122 crore in 2020– 21 to `1,89,892 crore in 2024–25, indicating a stronger focus on development and welfare expenditure.
  • Receipts from the central government increased from `91,625 crore in 2020–21 to `1,59,298 crore in 2024–25, mainly due to transfers from the divisible tax pool, which increased from `59,861 crore in 2020-21 to `1,29,435 crore in 2024-25.
  • In 2024: 25, grants-in-aid from the central government amounted to `29,863 crore.
  • The state’s own revenue increased from `36,543 crore in 2020–21 to `59,360 crore in 2024–25, with tax revenue as the major contributor.
  • As of 31 March 2025, the outstanding debt of the state government was `3,74,134 crore, which was 37.7 percent of the GSDP .
  • In 2024: 25, the number of Kisan Credit Card holders increased by 16.3 percent compared to 2023–24, while total credit disbursed increased by 32.8 percent from `7,080.07 crore in 2023–24 to `9,399.24 crore in 2024–25.
  • Total expenditure on irrigation in 2024–25 was `2,729.83 crore, of which 74.3 percent was capital expenditure. Between 2023–24 and 2024–25, total irrigation expenditure increased by 63.1 percent. This increase in public expenditure is reflected in the extensive expansion of major, medium, and minor irrigation projects.  Enterprise Sector
  • In 2024: 25, 37.8 lakh tourists visited 28 eco-tourism sites, which is 24.0 percent higher than the previous year. Total outlay for eco-tourism and park development in 2024–25 was `100.00 crore.  Human Development
  • Over the past 19 years, expenditure on social services has increased rapidly, rising fivefold in the first decade and 15-fold between 2005–06 and 2024–25. Expenditure on health and education increased by 14.8 times and 13.2 times respectively.
  • Between 2011: 12 and 2024–25, Bihar’s forestry and logging sector achieved a compound annual growth rate of 10.4 percent at current prices. The total value increased from `4,187 crore to `15,139 crore during this period.
  • Bihar’s commissioned power capacity is 11,764 MW , with the major share coming from thermal power (7,743 MW). Solar energy is the largest renewable source with a contribution of 1,922 MW , followed by hydropower at 1,356 MW and wind energy at 699 MW .
  • In 2024: 25, the total budget allocation for the Urban Development and Housing Department was `5,274.9 crore and total expenditure was `3,613.8 crore, with a utilisation rate of 68.5 percent.

  • The 20th edition was hosted in Aichi-Nagoya (Japan), from September 19 to October 4, 2026.
  • Nagoya, the Capital City of Aichi Prefecture, is the third Japanese city to host the Asian Games, after Tokyo in 1958 and Hiroshima in 1994.
  • ‘Honohon’ is the official mascot of the 20 th Asian Games, meaning ‘flame’ in Japanese.
  • The official Slogan, “Imagine One Asia”, emphasises unity, friendship and shared sporting aspirations across the Asian continent.
  • A total of 46 teams participated in Aichi-Nagoya Games (45 National Olympic Committees and the OCA Refugee Team).
  • The Asian Games 2026 featured 43 sports.
  • It was India’s second: highest medal tally at the Asian Games after the 2022 edition.
  • Pranavi urs became the first Indian woman to win an individual golf gold medal.
